When she started to notice that she was no longer enjoying her technical hobbies and that she was making small but consequential mistakes at work because she could not focus on even simple schematics, her manager suggested the employee assistance program, which led to a psychiatric referral.\n- Chief Complaint: “I used to be able to focus for hours on designs, but now my mind feels foggy and I errors were minor and already fixed.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Every so often when I’m alone in my apartment or sitting at my desk late, I hear this calm, almost clinical voice in my head telling me better off if I weren’t dragging the team down.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Where I used to be able to zone in on a design problem for hours, now my brain feels like it’s full of static—I reread the same line on a drawing five times, can’t decide something as simple as which tolerance scheme to use, and even picking what to eat for dinner can paralyze me for fifteen minutes.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On bad nights, when I’m lying there thinking about how I’ve supposedly ruined everything, I catch myself wishing I just wouldn’t wake up, and I’ve even found myself picturing in disappear.\n- functional_impairment: Over the past year I’ve gone from being one of the more reliable people on the team to missing internal deadlines, needing coworkers to double-check simple drawings, and turning down almost all social plans because I’m either too exhausted or too wrapped up in worrying about work to function normally outside the job.\n- duration: Ever since a few months after I started the full-time position on this aerospace project—so for a little over a year now—I’ve felt this heavy depression with the guilt, foggy thinking, and scary thoughts about being to blame errors were minor and already fixed.\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Every so often when I’m alone in my apartment or sitting at my desk late, I hear this calm, almost clinical voice in my head telling me the same line on a drawing five times, can’t decide something as simple as which tolerance scheme to use, and even picking what to eat for dinner can paralyze me for fifteen minutes.\n- S032 disappear.\n- functional_impairment: Over the past year I’ve gone from being one of the more reliable people on the team to missing internal deadlines, needing coworkers to double-check simple I've even had detailed thoughts about driving my car off the overpass on my commute. I haven't acted on any of those thoughts, but they are really scary.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a similar period of feeling very down, exhausted, and losing interest in your hobbies before this current episode started a year ago?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never experienced anything like this before. This is the very first time I’ve ever felt this way, and it’s completely different from how I used to be. Before this past year, I was always able to handle my work and enjoy my life.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or weight since these feelings started, such as eating much less or more than usual?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any significant changes in my appetite or weight. However, actually deciding what to eat for dinner has become a huge struggle that can paralyze me for fifteen minutes. My mind just feels too foggy to make even simple choices like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since this started, such as having trouble falling asleep, waking up in the middle of the night, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I haven't really tracked my sleep patterns specifically. I tried to cope at first by working late nights and weekends to keep up, but the constant stress and heavy workload eventually wore me down. I think that's what triggered these major issues with my focus and decision-making.\n\nDoctor: Along with the difficulty focusing, have you been feeling persistently down or lost interest in activities you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've been feeling really down, and the things I used to love just feel completely flat and pointless now. I used to get excited about tinkering with 3-D printers or going on long runs with my track club, but now I just end up sitting on my couch scrolling through old emails instead.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this down and lacking interest in your hobbies, and has this been happening nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yes, I've felt this way nearly every single day for a little over a year now, starting not long after I transitioned to the full-time position. For months, hobbies like running with my track club or tinkering with 3-D printers have just felt completely flat and pointless.\n\nDoctor: Besides feeling down, have you also been experiencing constant, excessive worry or anxiety about work or other areas of your life that you find hard to control?\n\nPatient: Yes, I'm constantly consumed by this intense worry about work that I just can't seem to control. I lie awake at night replaying every tiny mistake, terrified that I've ruined the entire project. I've even become convinced that my boss is secretly gathering evidence to hold me personally responsible when something goes wrong.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned intense worry about work. Do you also find yourself constantly worrying about other areas of your life, like your health, family, finances, or even minor daily matters?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My mind is almost entirely consumed by my work, the mistakes I've made, and the fear of being held responsible for them. I don't really have the mental space to worry about my health, family, or finances right now.\n\nDoctor: Along with feeling down, have you been experiencing a significant loss of energy or feeling constantly exhausted, even without physical exertion?\n\nPatient: Yes, I feel completely exhausted all the time, even though I'm not doing heavy physical labor. My body constantly feels like it's moving through molasses, and I'm usually too drained to handle social plans or function normally outside of work.\n\nDoctor: When you are feeling this low, have you had any thoughts of hurting yourself or felt that life is no longer worth living?\n\nPatient: On bad nights when I feel overwhelmed by guilt, I catch myself wishing I just wouldn’t wake up. I've even had detailed thoughts about driving my car off the overpass on my commute. I haven't acted on any of those thoughts, but they are really scary.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced a similar period of feeling very down, exhausted, and losing interest in your hobbies before this current episode started a year ago?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never experienced anything like this before. This is the very first time I’ve ever felt this way, and it’s completely different from how I used to be. Before this past year, I was always able to handle my work and enjoy my life.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your appetite or weight since these feelings started, such as eating much less or more than usual?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any significant changes in my appetite or weight. However, actually deciding what to eat for dinner has become a huge struggle that can paralyze me for fifteen minutes. My mind just feels too foggy to make even simple choices like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since this started, such as having trouble falling asleep, waking up in the middle of the night, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: I haven't really tracked my sleep patterns specifically. Other than lying awake some nights worrying about work, I'm not really sure if there's been a big change.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt the opposite of this—with extremely high energy, needing very little sleep, or feeling unusually happy and 'on top of the world'?\n\nPatient: No, I've never experienced anything like that. Even on my rare better days, my energy and mood only ever get closer to a baseline normal, never elevated or unusually high. I have definitely never felt 'on top of the world.'\n\nDoctor: Do you have any underlying medical conditions, or do you use alcohol, medications, or other substances? I want to make sure we rule out any physical causes for how you've been feeling."
